91: /** Is `id` a registered mod? @param {unknown} id */
92: export const isModId = (id) => typeof id === 'string' && Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(MODS, id);
93: 
94: /**
95:  * The default set of enabled mods: every registered mod (the owner's decision of 2026-10-09 — an install that never
96:  * mentions mods plays the full 0.3.0 content). @type {ReadonlySet<string>}
97:  */
98: export const DEFAULT_MODS = Object.freeze(new Set(MOD_IDS));
99: 
100: /**
101:  * Normalise a mod set: unknown ids are dropped, everything else kept. Returns a frozen array in registry order, which
102:  * is what the wire and `room.state` carry (a stable order keeps `room.state` comparisons and tests readable).
103:  * `null` / `undefined` means "nothing was said" and yields the defaults; an explicit empty array means "every mod off"
104:  * (the two are different, so a caller can express either).
105:  * @param {unknown} value an array of ids, or a Set, or null / undefined
106:  * @returns {readonly string[]}
107:  */
108: export function normalizeMods(value) {
109:   if (value == null) return Object.freeze([...DEFAULT_MODS]);
110:   if (value instanceof Set) return Object.freeze(MOD_IDS.filter((id) => value.has(id)));
111:   const list = Array.isArray(value) ? value : [];
112:   return Object.freeze(MOD_IDS.filter((id) => list.includes(id)));
113: }
114: 
115: /**
116:  * A room's mods as a lookup Set (the shape filterData and the pickers take). `null` / `undefined` = the defaults.
117:  * @param {unknown} value
118:  * @returns {ReadonlySet<string>}
119:  */
120: export function modSet(value) {
121:   return value instanceof Set ? value : new Set(normalizeMods(value));
122: }
123: 
124: /**
125:  * Parse a mod command-line / environment value. The owner asked for one id per mod, switchable one at a time, so the
126:  * syntax is a comma list of ids with an optional prefix:
127:  *   "all" / "" (empty)      every mod on (the default)
128:  *   "none"                  every mod off
129:  *   "custom-ops"            exactly that one (a plain list is an allow-list)
130:  *   "-custom-ops"           everything but that one
131:  *   "+custom-ops"           all plus that one (explicit, same as the plain form for a default-on registry)
132:  * Unknown ids are returned as `unknown` for the caller to report (a typo must never silently do nothing).
133:  * @param {unknown} raw
134:  * @returns {{ mods: readonly string[], unknown: string[], form: 'all'|'none'|'list'|'diff' }}
135:  */
136: export function parseMods(raw) {
137:   const text = String(raw ?? '').trim();
138:   if (!text || text === 'all' || text === '*') return { mods: normalizeMods(null), unknown: [], form: 'all' };
139:   if (text === 'none' || text === '-all' || text === 'off') return { mods: Object.freeze([]), unknown: [], form: 'none' };
140:   const parts = text.split(',').map((s) => s.trim()).filter(Boolean);
141:   const unknown = [];
142:   const negatives = parts.filter((p) => p.startsWith('-'));
143:   const positives = parts.filter((p) => !p.startsWith('-'));
144:   if (negatives.length) {
145:     const off = new Set();
146:     for (const part of negatives) {
147:       const id = part.slice(1);
148:       if (!isModId(id)) { unknown.push(id); continue; }
149:       off.add(id);
150:     }
151:     for (const part of positives) {
152:       const id = part.replace(/^\+/, '');
153:       if (!isModId(id)) unknown.push(id);
154:       else off.delete(id);
155:     }
156:     return { mods: Object.freeze(MOD_IDS.filter((id) => !off.has(id))), unknown, form: 'diff' };
157:   }
158:   const on = new Set();
159:   for (const part of positives) {
160:     const id = part.replace(/^\+/, '');
161:     if (!isModId(id)) unknown.push(id);
162:     else on.add(id);
163:   }
164:   return { mods: Object.freeze(MOD_IDS.filter((id) => on.has(id))), unknown, form: 'list' };
165: }
166: 
167: /** The ids every registered mod that the enabled set leaves off, in registry order. @param {unknown} mods */
168: export const disabledMods = (mods) => {
169:   const set = modSet(mods);
170:   return MOD_IDS.filter((id) => !set.has(id));
171: };
172: 
173: /**
174:  * Whether a mod is on in an enabled set (null / undefined = the defaults). @param {unknown} mods @param {string} id
175:  */
176: export const modOn = (mods, id) => modSet(mods).has(id);
177: 
178: /**
179:  * Whether any registered mod is switched off (the server says so once at boot; docs/MODS.md).
180:  * @param {unknown} mods
181:  */
182: export const anyModOff = (mods) => disabledMods(mods).length > 0;
183: 
184: /**
185:  * The mod-owned records of a data set: what tools/build-data.mjs stamped into `data/backups.json` `mods`, read back as
186:  * `{ units: Set, operators: Set, tokens: Set, skills: Set }` of every id any mod owns (the union, not per mod — see
187:  * filterData). An absent / malformed stamp reads as empty sets, so an un-stamped data set filters to nothing.
188:  * @param {any} backups data/backups.json
189:  */
190: export function modMarks(backups) {
191:   const out = { units: new Set(), operators: new Set(), tokens: new Set(), skills: new Set() };
192:   const marks = backups && typeof backups === 'object' ? backups.mods : null;
193:   if (!marks || typeof marks !== 'object' || Array.isArray(marks)) return out;
194:   for (const def of Object.values(marks)) {
195:     if (!def || typeof def !== 'object') continue;
196:     for (const key of ['units', 'operators', 'tokens', 'skills']) {
197:       if (!Array.isArray(def[key])) continue;
198:       for (const id of def[key]) if (typeof id === 'string' && id) out[key].add(id);
199:     }
200:   }
201:   return out;
202: }
203: 
204: /** Whether a data set carries any mod mark at all (a build stamped by tools/build-data.mjs). @param {any} backups */
205: export function hasModMarks(backups) {
206:   const m = modMarks(backups);
207:   return !!(m.units.size || m.operators.size || m.tokens.size || m.skills.size);
208: }
209: 
210: /**
211:  * Remove one mod's content from a data set. Three shapes are filtered, all keyed by the `data` object the server and the
212:  * browser already read (`{ chess, backups, tokens, assets, … }`):
213:  *   * `backups.units` / `backups.diy.operators` / `backups.diy.ownedPool` / `backups.tokens` — the custom operator's
214:  *     body, its 自选 operator record, its slot in the pool and its summons. `ownedPool` is also the picker's list, so
215:  *     removing it there is what takes the operator out of 自选编队 for every player.
216:  *   * `assets.chars` — its avatar / portrait / Spine alias (tools/custom-assets.mjs).
217:  *   * `assets.skills` + `assets.skillsById` — its skill icons, whose ids are their own (`skchr_<codename>_<n>`), never
218:  *     the charId, so the stamp names them (`skills`).
219:  * Only the ids this mod's mark names are touched; the rest of the data is returned as it was. The input is never
220:  * mutated (the server's data is deep-frozen and shared): a filtered copy is built only when something is removed.
221:  * @param {any} data `{ backups, assets, … }` (a plain data object, not a GameData / DataSource)
222:  * @param {string} modId
223:  * @returns {any} the same reference when nothing was removed, else a shallow copy with filtered sub-objects
224:  */
225: function applyCustomOps(data, modId) {
226:   const backups = data && typeof data === 'object' ? data.backups : null;
227:   if (!backups || typeof backups !== 'object') return data;
228:   const marks = backups.mods && typeof backups.mods === 'object' ? backups.mods[modId] : null;
229:   if (!marks || typeof marks !== 'object') return data;
230:   const units = new Set(Array.isArray(marks.units) ? marks.units : []);
231:   const operators = new Set(Array.isArray(marks.operators) ? marks.operators : []);
232:   const tokens = new Set(Array.isArray(marks.tokens) ? marks.tokens : []);
233:   const skills = new Set(Array.isArray(marks.skills) ? marks.skills : []);
234:   if (!units.size && !operators.size && !tokens.size && !skills.size) return data;
235: 
236:   const pick = (obj, drop) => {
237:     if (!obj || typeof obj !== 'object') return obj;
238:     const out = {};
239:     for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(obj)) if (!drop.has(k)) out[k] = v;
240:     return out;
241:   };
242:   const diy = backups.diy && typeof backups.diy === 'object' ? backups.diy : null;
243:   const nextDiy = diy ? {
244:     ...diy,
245:     ...(Array.isArray(diy.ownedPool) ? { ownedPool: diy.ownedPool.filter((id) => !units.has(id)) } : null),
246:     ...(diy.operators && typeof diy.operators === 'object' ? { operators: pick(diy.operators, operators) } : null),
247:   } : diy;
248:   const nextBackups = {
249:     ...backups,
250:     ...(backups.units && typeof backups.units === 'object' ? { units: pick(backups.units, units) } : null),
251:     ...(backups.tokens && typeof backups.tokens === 'object' ? { tokens: pick(backups.tokens, tokens) } : null),
252:     ...(nextDiy ? { diy: nextDiy } : null),
253:   };
254:   const out = { ...data, backups: nextBackups };
255: 
256:   const assets = data.assets && typeof data.assets === 'object' ? data.assets : null;
257:   if (assets) {
258:     out.assets = {
259:       ...assets,
260:       ...(assets.chars && typeof assets.chars === 'object' ? { chars: pick(assets.chars, units) } : null),
261:       ...(assets.skills && typeof assets.skills === 'object' ? { skills: pick(assets.skills, skills) } : null),
262:       ...(assets.skillsById && typeof assets.skillsById === 'object' ? { skillsById: pick(assets.skillsById, skills) } : null),
263:     };
264:   }
265:   return out;
266: }
267: 
268: /**
269:  * Remove the FIXED (固定) original operators from a data set: they live in `data/chess.json` (a PRESET chess each, both
270:  * forms) and in `data/garrisons.json` (their 特质), plus the two `assets` shapes the 自制干员 filter also trims (their
271:  * aliased avatar and skill icons). The stamp is `backups.mods['custom-fixed']` with `chess` (the chess ids) / `units`
272:  * (the charIds, for the art) / `skills` (the aliased skill icon ids).
273:  *
274:  * A removed chess takes its 特质 with it (the garage rows are keyed by id, `garrisons`): leaving them would let a 特质
275:  * with no owner linger in `bond`/`garrison` derived views. The shop pool, the shop itself and every picker read
276:  * `chess`, so trimming it there is what takes the operator out of the pool for the whole room.
277:  * @param {any} data `{ chess, garrisons, backups, assets, … }`
278:  * @param {string} modId
279:  * @returns {any} the same reference when nothing was removed, else a shallow copy with filtered sub-objects
280:  */
281: function applyCustomFixed(data, modId) {
282:   const backups = data && typeof data === 'object' ? data.backups : null;
283:   const marks = backups && backups.mods && typeof backups.mods === 'object' ? backups.mods[modId] : null;
284:   if (!marks || typeof marks !== 'object') return data;
285:   const chess = new Set(Array.isArray(marks.chess) ? marks.chess : []);
286:   const units = new Set(Array.isArray(marks.units) ? marks.units : []);
287:   const skills = new Set(Array.isArray(marks.skills) ? marks.skills : []);
288:   if (!chess.size && !units.size && !skills.size) return data;
289: 
290:   const pick = (obj, drop) => {
291:     if (!obj || typeof obj !== 'object') return obj;
292:     const out = {};
293:     for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(obj)) if (!drop.has(k)) out[k] = v;
294:     return out;
295:   };
296:   const out = { ...data };
297:   // the chess records, and the 特质 only they own (a garrison another chess also names stays)
298:   if (data.chess && typeof data.chess === 'object') {
299:     const kept = pick(data.chess, chess);
300:     // a garrison id is dropped only when every chess that named it is gone
301:     const stillUsed = new Set();
302:     for (const c of Object.values(kept)) for (const g of (c && Array.isArray(c.garrisonIds) ? c.garrisonIds : [])) stillUsed.add(g);
303:     const droppedGarrisons = new Set();
304:     for (const id of chess) for (const g of (data.chess[id]?.garrisonIds || [])) if (!stillUsed.has(g)) droppedGarrisons.add(g);
305:     out.chess = kept;
306:     if (data.garrisons && typeof data.garrisons === 'object' && droppedGarrisons.size) out.garrisons = pick(data.garrisons, droppedGarrisons);
307:   }
308:   const assets = data.assets && typeof data.assets === 'object' ? data.assets : null;
309:   if (assets && (units.size || skills.size)) {
310:     out.assets = {
311:       ...assets,
312:       ...(assets.chars && typeof assets.chars === 'object' ? { chars: pick(assets.chars, units) } : null),
313:       ...(assets.skills && typeof assets.skills === 'object' ? { skills: pick(assets.skills, skills) } : null),
314:       ...(assets.skillsById && typeof assets.skillsById === 'object' ? { skillsById: pick(assets.skillsById, skills) } : null),
315:     };
316:   }
317:   return out;
318: }
319: 
320: /**
321:  * Filter a data set by an enabled mod set: every disabled mod's `apply` runs in registry order. The input is not
322:  * mutated, and the SAME reference comes back when no mod removes anything (the common case: every mod on), so a caller
323:  * can cheaply tell "nothing changed" (`===`) and skip rebuilding anything derived from the data.
324:  * @param {any} data `{ chess, backups, assets, … }` or null
325:  * @param {unknown} mods an id array / Set, or null / undefined for the defaults
326:  * @returns {any}
327:  */
328: export function filterData(data, mods) {
329:   if (!data || typeof data !== 'object') return data;
330:   const enabled = modSet(mods);
331:   let out = data;
332:   for (const id of MOD_IDS) {
333:     if (enabled.has(id)) continue;
334:     out = MODS[id].apply(out, id);
335:   }
336:   return out;
337: }
338: 
339: /**
340:  * The mods a data set was actually stamped with (what it carries content for), in registry order — the server's
341:  * `/healthz` report and the boot log. A mod with no data of its own is reported too: the stamp says what the build
342:  * contains, and the registry says what exists. Null when the data has no stamp at all.
343:  * @param {any} data
344:  * @returns {readonly string[] | null}
345:  */
346: export function modsInData(data) {
347:   const backups = data && typeof data === 'object' ? data.backups : null;
348:   const marks = backups && typeof backups === 'object' ? backups.mods : null;
349:   if (!marks || typeof marks !== 'object' || Array.isArray(marks)) return null;
350:   return Object.freeze(MOD_IDS.filter((id) => Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(marks, id)));
351: }

Excellent. Now I have the key architectural insight for the plan:

**Our tree already has a "content mods" layer** (`shared/mods.js`, 351 lines) with:
- registry `MODS` (id, name, englishName, summary, since, hidden, apply)
- `filterData(data, mods)` — the seam (server/lobby.js:1522-1535 `dataFor(room)` filters per room; client public/js/data.js:308-323 filters too)
- room-level switch (`room.mods`), `SP_MODS` env override, lobby picker (`VISIBLE_MOD_IDS`), tests (`test/mods.test.js`, `test/ui/mods-ui.test.js`)
- data stamps in `data/backups.json` `mods` ({modId: {units, operators, tokens, skills, chess}}), because released installs don't ship `tools/`
- and docs/MODS.md:122-126 explicitly says: **content packs (`packs/<id>/`) are the future distribution mechanism, this mechanism is its simplified precursor; when the `data` type is really built, `filterData` is where pack data gets loaded, and the room switch can be reused.**

So the port plan should build on OUR existing mods layer rather than importing Forge's room-scoped subset machinery. That's the crucial finding: we already have "per-room content selection + server-authoritative data filtering + client mirroring" — which is exactly what Forge's `modIds`/roomAssets/room-data accomplish (though ours filters per room at the data-outlet level rather than materializing per-digest).

Wait — but there's an important difference: our mods are *registered in code* (a closed registry in shared/mods.js), so a mod = an id + an apply() filter. Third-party content packs would need to be *discovered from disk* and registered dynamically, plus their data merged *in*. Forge's direction is the opposite: packs ADD data (`applyWorkshop` merges in), our mods SUBTRACT data (`filterData` removes when off). Both are needed: a pack adds content; the room switch decides which packs are on.

So the design synthesis for our tree:
- Discovery/validation/normalization: port from Forge's `shared/workshop.js` (3,744 lines → a data-only subset) + `server/packFiles.js` (79) + `shared/modIdentity.js` (210 → canonicalJson + modManifestDigest + isModId... careful: our shared/mods.js already has `isModId` for the *registry*; naming collision! Forge's `isModId` is for pack ids. Need distinct names, e.g. `isPackId`.)
- Merge: `applyWorkshop(out, packs)` equivalent → merge pack content files into the data object BEFORE deepFreeze (our server/data.js:81 `deepFreeze(out)` — insert an overlay hook there exactly like Forge's server/data.js:89-107).

Actually for packs, the room switch semantic is inverted: default OFF for third-party packs (unlike our built-in mods which default ON). Important design point! Our DEFAULT_MODS = all registered mods on; a third-party pack should default OFF (a server install shouldn't force content on every room... actually Forge defaults to "all installed packs" for a plain room and lets a room declare a subset). Hmm: Forge's rule — a room that doesn't declare a set gets the process-level "official + all installed packs" body; a room CAN declare a subset. So default = all installed packs on. Our built-in mods also default ON. So consistent.

But our `filterData`-style model can't ADD pack content per room; it would need a per-room "materialize data = official + named packs". That's Forge's roomAssets (materialize by digest, cached, frozen). In our tree, `dataFor(room)` (server/lobby.js:1522) is exactly the seam where per-room data is produced: currently `filterData(base, room.mods)`. A data-pack port would make it `filterData(applyPacks(base, room.packIds), room.mods)`.

Also our client already fetches per-room data? public/js/data.js:308-323 does client-side `filterData` on the raw files it downloaded from `/data/*.json`. For packs, the client would need the merged data too — our client filters locally from the official data; for pack content it can't (it doesn't have the pack files). Forge solves this with `/room-data/<digest>/<file>.json`. So a port needs either:
 (a) server-side materialization per room + a route to serve it (client fetches `/room-data/...` instead of `/data/...`) — Forge's approach, or
 (b) simpler: keep serving `/data/*.json` but let the server inject pack content into the served payload for rooms... but the client's data fetch is per-room already? Let me check: does our client fetch data per room (i.e. with the room's mods in the query) or once globally? public/js/data.js filters locally using `mods` from room state. The download itself is global (`/data/x.json`) then filtered locally. So for packs we'd need the server to serve the merged+filtered data for a room (a room-scoped data route) — that's the (a) approach, and it's a real chunk of work (Forge's server/http/mods.js + roomAssets.js + client sync/align ~ 800 lines + protocol changes).

Alternatively (b'): since a pack is server-side content and the browser needs the same bytes for its deterministic sim, we could serve pack content as additional files and have the client merge them with the same rules (that's Forge's catalog+sync, minus the digest gate). Simpler but still needs a client merge implementation (shared/workshop.js runs in the browser too — Forge's shared/workshop.js is shared for this reason!). Since Forge's `shared/workshop.js` is shared code (3,744 lines, no Node builtins?), maybe we can reuse it wholesale in the browser and have the client merge packs fetched from a `/packs/<id>/...` route. That's essentially Forge's design without the digest/catalog sophistication.

Hmm. This is the crux of the plan: decide the *distribution/consumption* model. Options:
- **Model A (server-only packs, no client involvement)**: pack content is merged server-side; the client gets it by fetching the SAME data route it already uses, but the *server* must serve room-specific data. Requires: a room-scoped data route + client switching to it. Moderate.
- **Model B (client merges pack files)**: client downloads pack JSON files and runs the same merge code; no new data route, but the client needs the merge code (shared) + a fetch of pack files. Also moderate; and it lets the browser's sim data match.
- **Model C (packs are baked into the served data at boot, ignoring per-room selection)**: simplest: at startup, merge all packs into the data singleton; every room sees them; the room switch (our mods) can turn off a *registered* pack mod by id. Key: our client fetches `/data/<file>.json` (public/js/data.js:131 base), and `/data/` is a static mount (tools/package.mjs:250 URL_MOUNTS). There's no room-scoped data route. `server/lobby.js:1529 dataFor(room)` is server-side only. And the client applies `filterData` locally (public/js/data.js:308-323).

So for packs, if we want the browser to have the same content, either:
- serve pack content statically (e.g. `/workshop/<id>/...` read-only) and have the client merge with the same shared code, or
- add a room-scoped merged data route.

Given our client already filters locally by room mods, the natural extension: server exposes each installed pack's data files read-only, client fetches the packs the room enables and merges (shared code), then filters by mods. Same code path as the server → same bytes → deterministic sim agreement. This is Model B, and it reuses our existing "client owns a copy and filters it" architecture. Note: the server must also guarantee that a client with a *different* pack set can't join (digest/version check) — that's where Forge's digest gate comes in; phase 3.

<path>C:\Users\hhyyl\Downloads\Stronghold-Protocol-v0.2.1\Stronghold-Protocol-repo\docs\PACKS.md</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: # PACKS.md — content packs
2: 
3: A **content pack** is add-on content a server picks up from a folder: a manifest plus its files. The owner's decision of
4: 2026-10-07 made the language pack the first type of one general mechanism, so that later resource packs (art, audio,
5: fonts) and server-side data packs reuse the same manifest, registry and index instead of each inventing its own. In
6: 0.2.0 one type loads, **`lang`** (a language: docs/I18N.md "Adding a language"); `assets` and `data` are planned.
7: 
8: | File | Role |
9: |---|---|
10: | `shared/packs.js` | the format: the manifest, the pack types (`PACK_TYPES`), app version ranges, the index (browser, server and tools) |
11: | `shared/i18nPacks.js` | the `lang` type: language codes, the language fields, the fallback chain, the script family |
12: | `server/packs.js` | the registry: discovery, validation, the index, which pack files may be served |
13: | `server/http/static.js` | `GET /packs/index.json` (the live index) and `GET /packs/<id>/<file>` |
14: | `public/js/ui/lang.js` | the client of the `lang` entries: the language menu and the loader |
15: | `tools/packs.mjs` | `list`, `index` (the index file for a static host), `check` |
16: | `tools/package.mjs` | ships the committed packs and writes `packs/index.json` into the release zips |
17: 
18: ## 1. Layouts
19: 
20: Two layouts, read by the same registry and listed alike:
21: 
22: ```
23: packs/<id>/pack.json + its files      a folder pack — any type; the manifest names its files
24: public/i18n/<code>.json               a single-file language pack — its manifest is the file's `_meta` block
25: data/i18n/<code>.json                   (+ its game texts, when present)
26: ```
27: 
28: The single file is the quickest way for a translator (one file, the format English has always had, and how English
29: ships); the folder is the layout every pack type shares — a pack distributed as one folder with its own readme and
30: licence, and the only layout a resource or data pack will have. A language can come either way; the menu does not tell
31: them apart.
32: 
33: The id of a folder pack is its folder name; the id of a single-file language pack is its language code. Ids are
34: letters, digits, `.`, `_` and `-` (at most 64), unique across types and without regard to case (Windows and macOS
35: would see one folder).
36: 
37: ## 2. The manifest
38: 
39: `packs/<id>/pack.json`, or the `_meta` block of `public/i18n/<code>.json` (the same fields):
40: 
41: ```json
42: {
43:   "type": "lang",
44:   "lang": "ja",
45:   "name": "日本語",
46:   "englishName": "Japanese",
47:   "version": "1.0.0",
48:   "app": ">=0.2.0",
49:   "authors": ["…"],
50:   "credits": "…",
51:   "license": "CC-BY-4.0",
52:   "fallback": ["en"],
53:   "files": { "ui": "ui.json", "data": "data.json" }
54: }
55: ```
56: 
57: | Field | Meaning |
58: |---|---|
59: | `id` | optional: the folder or file name decides; a manifest that names another id gets a warning |
60: | `type` | `lang` (a single-file language pack may leave it out); planned: `assets`, `data` |
61: | `version` | the pack's own version |
62: | `app` | the app versions it is made for, a range: `>=0.2.0`, `>=0.2.0 <0.3.0`, `0.2.x`, `^0.2.0`, `~0.2.1`, `*`, alternatives with `||`. A pre-release (0.2.0-dev) counts as its release. Outside the range a language pack still loads — what it lacks falls back — and is flagged `compatible: false` (the server log, `tools/packs.mjs list`); a later type decides what an incompatible pack means for it |
63: | `name`, `englishName` | the name in its own language (a language pack: the language's own name, shown in the menu) and in English (the menu's tooltip) |
64: | `authors`, `credits`, `license` | who made it; free text (`license`: an SPDX id or text) |
65: | `files` | a folder pack: role → path inside the folder. Each type lists its roles and their extensions (`lang`: `ui` required, `data` optional, both `.json`) |
66: | the type's fields | `lang`: `lang`, `base`, `fallback`, `complete`, `machineTranslated`, `numberUnits` (docs/I18N.md) |
67: | `machineTranslated` | `lang`, optional: `true` when the pack's UI strings come from a machine (a translation, or a conversion such as the Traditional Chinese pack's). The index entry carries it (only when true, like `complete`) and the client shows a note under the language switch in 设置 while that language is in use (`public/js/ui/lang.js` `machineTranslationNote`). A value other than `true` / `false` is a warning and reads as false |
68: 
69: A **problem** keeps a pack from loading (unknown type, no `lang`, a file missing or outside the folder, broken JSON, an
70: id or a language already taken — the language folders' files come first, then the folders by id); a **warning** does
71: not (an id that differs, an app range this version is outside of, an unknown file role). Both are printed when the
72: server starts or notices a change, and by `node tools/packs.mjs list`.
73: 
74: ## 3. Discovery and the index
75: 
76: The server's registry (`server/packs.js`) scans `public/i18n/`, `data/i18n/` and `packs/` when it starts and again
77: whenever something there changes: on a request for the index or a pack file it compares the names, sizes and times of
78: the files it reads (at most once a second, a few `stat` calls) and scans again if they differ. A pack dropped into a
79: folder shows on the next page load — no restart, no build step; a removed one leaves (a player whose stored language is
80: gone gets Chinese).
81: 
82: `GET /packs/index.json` (never cached) answers the packs of supported types:
83: 
84: ```json
85: { "version": 1, "app": "0.2.0", "packs": [
86:   { "id": "en", "type": "lang", "name": "English", "englishName": "English", "version": "0.2.0", "app": ">=0.2.0",
87:     "compatible": true, "authors": ["…"], "lang": "en", "fallback": [], "complete": true, "strings": 1017,
88:     "files": { "ui": "/i18n/en.json", "data": "/data/i18n/en.json" } } ] }
89: ```
90: 
91: `files` holds URLs on this server, so a client never builds a path itself. A client reads the entries of its type
92: (`readPackIndex(json, 'lang')`: an entry of another type, with a bad id or a URL that is not a path on this server is
93: dropped).
94: 
95: **A static host** cannot list folders: `node tools/packs.mjs index` writes the same body to `packs/index.json`
96: (git-ignored), and `tools/package.mjs` writes it into the release zips. The live server answers `/packs/index.json`
97: itself and ignores that file. Serving the game from plain files needs the server's URL layout: `public/` at `/`,
98: `data/` at `/data/`, `shared/` at `/shared/`, `server/sim/` at `/sim/` and `packs/` at `/packs/` (the game itself still
99: needs the Node server for its rooms; DEPLOY.md).
100: 
101: **What is served.** `GET /packs/<id>/<file>` answers only a file that the manifest of a loaded folder pack names under a
102: role of its type, with that role's extension, inside the folder once links are resolved — `pack.json`, a readme, an
103: HTML or script file in the folder are never served; a pack of a planned type serves nothing. A single-file language
104: pack's files are the language folders' own (`/i18n/<code>.json`, `/data/i18n/<code>.json`).
105: 
106: ## 4. The types
107: 
108: | Type | Status | Where it applies | Loader |
109: |---|---|---|---|
110: | `lang` | supported (0.2.0) | the client: UI strings, game texts | `public/js/ui/lang.js` (the menu, the strings: `shared/i18n.js`; the game texts: `public/js/data.js`) |
111: | `assets` | planned | the client: art, audio, font replacements | — |
112: | `data` | planned | the server: data patches applied at start | — |
113: 
114: A pack of a planned type is parsed and listed as not supported ("type "assets" is planned …, not loaded by this
115: version"); it is never served and never changes anything.
116: 
117: **Before a `data` pack, 内容 mod** ([docs/MODS.md](MODS.md)) is the simpler mechanism the remake ships: the content the
118: remake itself adds (`shared/mods.js` — 自制干员 first, 无尽模式 and the like later) is one **mod** per block, switched per
119: room by the host, removed from the data a room plays with when off. It is not a pack type — no manifest, no folder, the
120: content is committed in `data/` and stamped (`data/backups.json` `mods`) — but it keeps the four rules below and its
121: `filterData` is where a `data` pack's payload would be applied, so the room switch and the client filter carry over.
122: 
123: ### Adding a pack type
124: 
125: 1. **The type** — its entry in `PACK_TYPES` (`shared/packs.js`): `status`, `summary`, `live` (whether a running server
126:    may take changes without a restart) and `files` (the roles a folder pack names, with the extensions that may be
127:    served for each). Its own manifest fields get a normalizer beside `langFields` and a branch in `normalizeManifest`;
128:    its index fields a branch in `packIndexEntry`.
129: 2. **The loader** — reads the registry by type: `createPackRegistry(…).list('assets')` on the server, or the index
130:    entries of the type in the browser (`readPackIndex(json, 'assets')`), the way `ui/lang.js` reads `lang`. A
131:    server-side type (`data`) loads once at start (`live: false`): the simulation's code and data are fixed for the life
132:    of the process, and a battle must run the same data on the server and in every browser.
133: 3. **The checks** — what `tools/packs.mjs check` and the tests verify for the type (a data pack: the same validation
134:    the data builder applies; a resource pack: the files exist and are of the allowed kinds), and its documentation
135:    here.
136: 
137: Things later types must keep (the maintainers' study of mods, 2026-10-07):
138: 
139: - **Server-side only for gameplay.** A data pack is installed on the server, so every player of a room uses the same
140:   content and the server can still check or recompute a battle. A player never installs gameplay content of their own.
141: - **A content hash in the handshake** before data packs ship (`welcome`, `/healthz`, the BattleSpec), so a client and
142:   a server can tell they run the same content, and a "modded" mark in the UI and in bug reports.
143: - **No third-party code.** The manifest and its files are data (JSON, images, audio, fonts); a pack type that runs code
144:   is a separate decision of the owner's.
145: - **The golden results stay on the original content**; a pack never changes them.
146: 
147: ## 5. Tools
148: 
149: ```
150: node tools/packs.mjs list [--json]          the packs by type, what was skipped and why, the warnings
151: node tools/packs.mjs index [--out <file>]   write packs/index.json (the static index)
152: node tools/packs.mjs check [--strict]       list; --strict exits 1 when a pack is skipped or warned about
153: node tools/i18n.mjs template <code>         a new language pack (docs/I18N.md)
154: node tools/i18n.mjs check <code> | --all    a language pack's strings: coverage, placeholders
155: ```
156: 
157: `--root <dir>` points `tools/packs.mjs` at another checkout or an unpacked release.
158: 
159: ## 6. Shipping
160: 
161: `tools/package.mjs` ships the committed packs (`public/i18n/`, `data/i18n/`, `packs/` — tracked files only: a pack
162: installed on this machine and not committed stays out) and writes `packs/index.json` into the stage from the packs
163: that ship. `test/package.test.js` checks both layouts and that a local pack stays out.
164: 
165: ## Tests
166: 
167: `test/i18n-packs.test.js` (discovery of both layouts, broken / planned / duplicate packs, a pack dropped in or removed
168: while the server runs, the file allowlist, the manifest and app ranges, the `machineTranslated` mark from the manifest
169: to the 设置 note, the fallback chain, placeholders, plurals,
170: the per-text game-text fallback, the client loader and menu, the tools, build-i18n `--lang` / `--dict`),
171: `test/package.test.js`, `test/ui/i18n.e2e.test.js`
172: (`SP_E2E=1`: a pack file and a pack folder dropped in while the server runs show in the menu and switch; the shipped
173: packs with their UI strings and the 设置 note of a machine-translated one).

(End of file - total 173 lines)

- `PACK_TYPES` with `data: { status: 'planned', summary: 'data patches the server applies at start' }` (line 61). To turn it on: `status: 'supported'`, `live: false`, `files: {...roles}`.

So the port's shape:

**Phase 1 — make the `data` type real (server-side, boot-time, no code execution)**
1. `shared/packs.js`: `PACK_TYPES.data` → `{ status: 'supported', live: false, summary: ..., files: { <role>: { ext: ['.json'] } … } }`; add the data-type manifest fields to the typedef + `normalizeManifest` branch: `content` (roles→files, derived from `files`), `overrides: ["<file>:<id>"]`, maybe `api`/`game` (Forge's ABI/version range) — note our packs already have `app` ranges; Forge adds `game` (upstream version) + `api` (mod API). Decision: keep `app` only (our mechanism), skip `api` (no code → no ABI) — a simplification worth stating.
2. New `shared/dataPack.js` (or `shared/packsData.js`): the **data-only subset of Forge's shared/workshop.js**: closed role set + id-field map (`ID_FIELD_BY_FILE`: chess→chessId? our data uses `chessId`, bonds→id, …), record self-check, additive merge + `overrides` gating, `EMPTY_PACK`, unknown-field rejection, per-file normalizers, `applyDataPacks(base, packs)` → `{ data, report }`, and `dataPackSummary(report)`. Naming: our repo uses `shared/packs.js` + `shared/i18nPacks.js` → so `shared/dataPacks.js` fits perfectly ("the `data` type: data patches…").
3. `server/packs.js`: registry already lists by type; ensure the data type loads once at start (`live:false`), and serve only declared files (the allowlist already enforces `files` roles → a data pack's JSON files become fetchable at `/packs/<id>/<file>` — needed for the browser in Phase 2, harmless now).
4. `server/data.js`: add the overlay seam before `deepFreeze` (mirroring Forge's server/data.js:89-107): `if (packsDir) { const {data: merged, report} = applyDataPacks(out, dataPacks); … log }`. Our data.js has `getData({dir, log})`/`loadData(dir, {log, expected})` — add `packsDir`/`dataPacks` opts (and `null` to skip, for tests).
5. `tools/packs.mjs check/list`: a data pack's validation = the same normalizer + a dry-run merge; `--strict` fails on problems.
6. Digest + handshake (our docs require it): add `digest` to the index entry (`packIndexEntry`) computed by a new `dataPackDigest`/`canonicalJson` — Forge's `modManifestDigest` gives the algorithm: hash the sorted `{path, hash}` list of the pack's declared files (canonical JSON of each normalized record). Then surface it in `welcome`/`/healthz` and the BattleSpec (our own doc's requirement). Phase 1 can include digest computation + `/packs/index.json` exposure; the handshake wiring is small (protocol.js `welcome` validator + lobby).
7. Docs: `docs/PACKS.md` §4 (data supported + its roles/fields/checks/rules), `docs/MODS.md` (the relationship: pack content + room switch), `docs/DATA.md`? and CHANGELOG + docs-consistency tests.
8. Tests: `test/data-packs.test.js` (mirroring test/i18n-packs.test.js): discovery, manifest validation, unknown field rejection, override gating, additive merge, empty pack, digest stability, and an end-to-end `loadData({packsDir})` with a fixture pack; plus `test/mods.test.js`-style room-scope tests in Phase 2. Golden: unchanged (a pack never changes golden — our doc says so; keep packs out of the default test data path).

**Phase 2 — per-room selection + client parity**
- Register each loaded data pack as a room-switchable mod (our `shared/mods.js` registry is code-level; for packs we'd derive a dynamic registry entry, e.g. `pack:<id>`, with `apply` removing that pack's records when off, using a *stamp* mechanism like our existing `data/backups.json.mods` — or better: since the server has the pack's own record list, `apply` can remove exactly the ids the pack contributed, recorded at merge time).
  - Key idea: at merge time, build a per-pack index `{ file → ids added/overridden }`. Then a room with pack X off = data minus X's records (undo of its overlay), which is deterministic and requires no re-read. That's *inverse* of our current filterData model but works.
- Client parity: the browser must merge the same packs. Options: (a) fetch each enabled pack's JSON files from `/packs/<id>/<file>` (already allowlisted) and run the same `shared/dataPacks.js` merge client-side (our public/js/data.js already filters locally; add a merge step before filtering); (b) server-side room-scoped data route. Recommend (a) — reuses our existing client-side data ownership, no new route, and the digest (Phase 1) lets the client verify it got the same bytes.
- Handshake: `welcome`/`room.state` carry the pack digest set; the client refuses to ready up if its local pack bytes don't match (Forge's align.js analogue — but simpler: a fetch + digest check + a "重新加载" prompt).
- BattleSpec: include the digest so replays/spectators are unambiguous.

**Phase 3 (optional/later) — authoring convenience**: `tools/packs.mjs new/export/import` (zip). Requires a zip reader (our tree: need to check what package-update.mjs uses — subagent B will tell). Could shell to PowerShell? Better: port Forge's `shared/zip.js` (435 lines, needs refinement: ZIP64/encryption rejection, GBK names).

Explicit non-goals (never): `kits/` behavior scripts, `server.meta/modules/battle/room`, `client.panels`, `routes`, asset containers (`assets` type stays planned), skins/forms/fx, voices. Rationale: our docs/PACKS.md:143 "No third-party code" + MODS.md:124. Also: we do NOT need Forge's `modCatalog`/`sync`/`align` digest-and-download machinery in Phase 1/2 because our `/packs/index.json` + `/packs/<id>/<file>` allowlist already covers serving.

Risks:
1. **Determinism/handshake**: a client with a different pack set must not play; our doc already demands the content hash — do it in Phase 1 even if the client can't apply packs yet? If Phase 1 is server-only, the client would receive merged data via /data/*.json (since the server serves the merged data — wait, does it? Our server serves `data/` from disk statically (tools/package.mjs:250 URL_MOUNTS: '/data/' → 'data/'). So the client fetches the *raw files*, NOT the server's in-memory merged data! That's a crucial architectural fact: our server's data merging (getData + filterData) is invisible to the browser, which fetches the same files statically and filters locally. Forge solved this because... hmm, does Forge serve merged data? Forge has `/room-data/<digest>/<file>.json`. And for the *default* body, the client... let me think: Forge's client also fetches /data/*.json for the default set and merges packs client-side via mods/sync.js. Yes.
   ⇒ **Phase 1 server-only is NOT sufficient for browser parity**: pack content merged in the server's memory would NOT reach the browser (it fetches raw files). So any pack content (new chess etc.) would break the sim agreement: server sim knows the content, browser sim doesn't.
   ⇒ Therefore the minimum viable port MUST include client-side application: either (a) the client downloads + merges the packs (needs Phase 2 machinery), or (b) serve a merged data tree (a new route, e.g. `/data-merged/<digest>/<file>.json`, or write merged files to a temp dir and mount them). 
   Option (b) is simpler for parity: the server materializes the merged data body (official + packs) and serves it under a versioned URL prefix; the client's data base switches from `/data/` to that prefix. Our public/js/data.js takes `opts.base` (line 131: `const base = opts.base ?? '/data/'`) and public/js/battle/runner.js `loadBrowserSim({dataBase='/data/'})` — both already parameterized! So (b) is quite feasible: server computes the merged body at boot, writes it to a cache dir (or serves it from memory over a route), and the boot config tells the client which base to use. Our client's data base is set where? Need to check how public/js/data.js is constructed (maybe from a global config). If it's hardcoded `/data/`, we need to thread it through the boot payload.
   Alternatively (a): client fetches `/packs/index.json` + each data pack's files and merges locally with `shared/dataPacks.js` — no server materialization, no new route, symmetric code, and it aligns with our client's existing local filtering. Cost: the client needs the merge code (shared → fine) and a boot-time list of installed packs (index already gives it).
   I'd recommend (a) — same reasoning as Forge, and our docs' "content hash in the handshake" then verifies parity.

Where to write: `04-文档/工坊包数据层移植评估.md`. Let me compose a well-structured document:

Title: 工坊包（workshop pack）数据层移植评估 — 只带数据、不执行第三方代码

0. 结论摘要（TL;DR）: 推荐做；分两期；不需要搬 Forge 的 workshop/ 系统，而是把 Forge 的**数据规则**嫁接到我们已有的 packs/ 类型机制上；执行面（kits/server.*/client.panels/routes/assets）一律不搬，理由是我们自己的政策（docs/MODS.md:124、docs/PACKS.md:143）。
1. 背景与两个参考样本（社区覆盖式 mod vs Forge 包系统）+ 我们已有的地基（表格：packs 机制 / mods 机制 / 数据管线 seam / 静态路由）。
2. 目标与非目标（明确列出永不搬的格子）。
3. 设计：数据流图（服务端 + 浏览器），模块清单，接口签名草案。
4. 分期计划 P1 / P2（每期：改动文件、验收命令、风险）。
5. 工作量与文件级清单（表：文件、动作、估计行数）。
6. 与政策的冲突点与需要业主拍板的 3 件事（a: packs/ 是提交进 git 还是仅本机？Forge 的包不提交、我们的 packs 机制是提交+打包的；b: 第三方包的许可证与打包策略；c: 内容哈希握手是否 P1 就做）。
7. 风险清单（确定性/握手、golden、打包/更新、i18n、客户端一致性、zip 能力缺口、测试沙箱）。
8. 验收标准（每期 verify 命令）。

Key design decisions to state:
- 用我们已有的 `packs/` + `/packs/index.json` + `/packs/<id>/<file>`（allowlist），不建第二个 discovery/目录/索引系统（Forge 的 workshop/ + modCatalog + sync/align 在我们这里被 `/packs` 通道替代）。
- 每间房开关复用 `shared/mods.js` 的 room switch：把每个已装 data pack 变成一个动态注册的 mod（id 例如 `pack:<packId>`），关闭时从数据里摘掉它贡献的记录（合并时记下它加了/覆盖了哪些 id，形成 per-pack 索引）。
- 默认值：已装包默认开（和 Forge「默认本体含全部已装包」+ 我们「默认全开」一致）；`SP_MODS`/`--mods` 语法可复用（`none` 关掉全部）。
- 内容哈希：按 docs/PACKS.md:141-142 的要求，P1 就产出 digest 并进 `welcome` / `/healthz` / BattleSpec；客户端拿到包文件后自己重算并比对，不匹配就不给「准备」。
- Forge 的 ABI 概念（`api`/MOD_API_VERSION）我们**不要**：没有代码就没有 ABI；只保留 `app` 版本区间（已有）。
- `game`（上游游戏版本区间）可选搬。
- 素材（`assets` 容器/manifest、voices、skins、forms、fx、i18n 补词条）**不在 P1/P2**：`assets` 类型仍是 planned；若将来要做，也按「只读文件 + 声明」的方式，不做执行。
- 客户端的 `data/*.json` 与服务端内存数据**当前是两条路**（浏览器直接静态取 `/data/`，服务端在内存叠加）——这是移植必须解决的核心一致性问题（我核实过：tools/package.mjs:250 的 URL_MOUNTS 把 `/data/` 映射到 `data/`）。两个方案：(a) 浏览器自己拉包并本地合并（推荐，复用 public/js/data.js:131 的 base 与 :308-323 的 filterData 思路）；(b) 服务端物化合并体并加一条 room-scoped/带版本前缀的数据路由（改动更大）。选 (a)。

Effort estimate:
P1（服务端 + 共享 + 客户端本地合并 + 摘要，无房间子集）:
- shared/dataPacks.js 新增 ~600-900 行
- shared/packs.js +80~140（类型条目、data 字段规范化、index entry 带 digest）
- server/packs.js +60~120（类型感知装载、digest、校验）
- server/data.js +20~30（叠加 seam）
- public/js/data.js +40~80（拉 index + 包文件 + 合并）
- shared/protocol.js +1 字段（welcome 的 packs 摘要）+ 校验分支 ~20
- tools/packs.mjs +80~120（check/list 对 data 类型）
- docs/PACKS.md §4 + docs/MODS.md 交叉引用 + CHANGELOG
- test/data-packs.test.js 新增 ~350-500 行
合计算 ~1,300-1,900 行，不含 P2。

P2（房间子集 + 准备前对齐 + 打包策略）: +400-800 行。
P3（zip import/export + 编辑器式脚手架）: 需先补 zip 读取器（我们当前没有 shared/zip.js；tools/package-update.mjs 怎么解包待子代理结论）。

Risks:
- golden：包内容绝不能进 golden（我们的 doc 要求）→ 测试默认 packsDir=null。
- 打包：tools/package.mjs 现在「只带已提交的 packs」；第三方包不该进发布包 → 需要显式决定（gitignore + 不打包）。
- 依赖：不需要新依赖（纯 JSON 校验 + 已有 crypto）。
- 沙箱：node --test 在本机 spawn EPERM，验收用 `node <testfile>`。
- 一致性：客户端合并必须与服务端同码同序（同一 shared 模块 + 固定排序）。

Also: what about `units.json`→`backups.units` mapping (Forge) — our data has `data/backups.json` with `units`, and DIY. Our content roles should mirror our DATA_FILES: config? (probably exclude config = rules), chess, bonds, garrisons, items, bands, effects, choices, enemies, factions, waves, stages, bosses, tokens, backups(units), hextech? Also `assets` (data/assets.json is the art manifest — data, not files!). Hmm: our `data/assets.json` is the art *manifest* (paths), and pack art would be asset files → that's the `assets` type (planned). For P1, exclude `assets` from data roles (no art) → but then a new operator can't have art... The Forge handles art via `art` declarations + assets container. Our P1 = data only, so new chess/units without art → degraded (avatar-less). Acceptable and explicitly stated: a data pack in P1 brings rules/content, not art; art is the `assets` type (planned) or manual.

Wait — our mods system (`filterData`) touches `assets.chars`/`assets.skills` for custom ops, and those came from `tools/custom-ops.mjs` + committed assets. So a data pack overriding `assets.json` (the manifest) could point at art files... which would need to be served + allowed. That's the assets type. Keep out of scope, note it.

Also `choices.json`, `hextech.json` (our 0.3.0 file) — a pack could bring hex cards. Role list should be our DATA_FILES minus `config` (rules — Forge deliberately excludes config!) minus `assets` (art manifest) plus `hextech`. Good: mirror Forge's reasoning: "config 被刻意排除：一个能改写经济、回合表或难度参数的包改的是规则而不是内容" (WORKSHOP.md:92). Our `data/config.json` is exactly that → exclude. Note: our `DATA_FILES` in server/data.js:27-31 includes 'config' and we'd declare roles for the rest.

的「创意工坊包」系统。\n> 移植目标树：`Stronghold-Protocol-repo`（业主 0.3.0 分支，git HEAD = v0.2.3 merge，底座是上游 v0.2.4）。\n> 本文只做评估与方案，不含任何代码改动。行号与文件路径均已核对到具体位置。\n\n---\n\n## 0. 结论\n\n1. **值得做，但不该照搬 Forge 的 `workshop/` 系统。** Forge 的工坊是一整套平行设施（独立目录、独立注册表、`modCatalog`、客户端 `sync/align`、房间按摘要物化 `roomAssets`）。我们树里**已经有等价的两块地基**：\n   - 内容包机制（`packs/` + `shared/packs.js` 的类型表 + `server/packs.js` 注册表 + `GET /packs/index.json` / `GET /packs/<id>/<file>` 白名单服务）；\n   - 房间级内容开关 + 服务端权威数据过滤（`shared/mods.js` `filterData` + `server/lobby.js:1529 dataFor(room)` + 客户端 `public/js/data.js:308-323`）。\n   而我们自己的文档已经把路铺好了：`docs/PACKS.md:112` 写着 `data` 类型（planned，服务端启动时应用的数据补丁），`:117-121` 写着「做 data 包之前，内容 mod 是简化前置……**它的 `filterData` 就是 data 包载荷会被应用的位置**，房间开关与客户端过滤可以直接沿用」。\n2. **所以移植物是 Forge 的「数据规则」，不是它的设施**：内容文件规范化 + 只增不改 + `overrides` 显式白名单 + 顶层键闭集拒绝 + 记录自检 + 摘要身份。执行面（`kits/*.js`、`server.meta/modules/battle/room`、`client.panels`、`routes`、`assets` 容器、`voices/skins/forms/fx`）**一格都不搬**。\n3. **必须在 P1 就解决的一致性硬点**：我们的浏览器是**直接静态取 `data/*.json`**（`tools/package.mjs:250` 把 `/data/` 映射到 `data/`），而服务端是在内存里叠加/过滤（`server/data.js:81` 冻结、`server/lobby.js:1529` 按房间过滤）。**服务端内存里的包内容不会自动到达浏览器。** 因此「只做服务端」的最小子集是**不成立**的，P1 必须包含客户端的本地合并（推荐：浏览器拉 `/packs/index.json` + 包文件，用同一份 shared 代码合并）。\n4. 规模：P1 约 **1,300–1,900 行**改动/新增（其中共享规则模块 600–900 行，其余是类型表、注册表、数据管线 seam、客户端合并、工具、测试、文档），P2（房间子集 + 准备前对齐）再加 **400–800 行**。不引入任何新依赖。\n\n---\n\n## 1. 两个参照物，和我们的地基\n\n### 1.1 两个「加 mod」样本（业主这轮先后放进 `参考对象\\`）\n\n| 样本 | 机制 | 安全模型 |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `休整期切换技能模组mod（修复自定义干员无法切换）` | 用 `payload/` 里**整份文件覆盖**游戏源码（3 个文件），`install.mjs` 做 sha256 基线白名单 + 备份到 `.diy-loadout-backup/`，重启服务端 | 无加载器、无沙箱；冲突靠「不在白名单就拒绝」，装 mod = 往服务端进程里塞第三方源码 |\n| `Stronghold-Protocol-Forge-0.15.0` 的工坊包 | 包是**数据 + 声明**（`workshop/<id>/pack.json` + 内容文件 + `kits/` + 素材），装载器在启动时扫描、规范化、叠加；身份 = 目录全部普通文件的规范摘要；房间按 `modIds` 点名的子集物化；客户端按 `/mods/catalog.json` 逐文件下载并重算摘要 | 顶层键与 `server` 成员都是**闭集**，写不认识的键整包被点名拒绝；素材/客户端/服务端代码各自有声明与守卫（`docs/MOD-SURFACE.md`、`shared/modSurface.js`、`shared/kitImports.js` 受限 import 白名单）；**房间点名才生效** |\n\nForge 的「执行面」是真实存在的（`kits/`、`server.meta`、`server.modules`、`server.battle`、`server.room`、`client.panels`、`routes`），本评估**全部排除**，理由是我们的政策原文：\n\n- `docs/MODS.md:124`：「**不执行第三方代码**：mod 是数据与注册表条目，不是插件脚本。」\n- `docs/PACKS.md:143-144`：「**No third-party code.** The manifest and its files are data (JSON, images, audio, fonts); a pack type that runs code is a separate decision of the owner's.」\n- `docs/PACKS.md:139-140`：玩法内容**只装服务端**，玩家自己装不了玩法内容。\n- `docs/PACKS.md:145`：**golden 结果永远基于原生内容**，包永不改 golden。\n- `docs/PACKS.md:141-142`：**上线 data 包之前要有内容哈希进握手**（`welcome`、`/healthz`、BattleSpec），并在 UI 与 bug 报告里标「已加装内容」。\n\n### 1.2 我们已有的地基（可直接复用，不必新建）\n\n| 地基 | 位置 | 现状 |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| 包类型表 | `shared/packs.js:55-62` | `PACK_TYPES = { lang: supported/live, assets: planned, data: planned }`；`data` 的类型条目已存在，只需从 `planned` 变 `supported` 并补 `files` 角色 |\n| 清单规范化 | `shared/packs.js:151 normalizeManifest(raw, ctx)`，`lang` 分支在 `:175` | 加一个 `type === 'data'` 分支即可（未知类型/未知角色/坏 JSON/重复 id 包身份覆盖**目录里每一个普通文件**（`README.md`、`*.bak`、点文件都算），因为「被声明的模块可以 import 同目录的兄弟文件」。**这一条在我们这里必须改写**：我们不执行包代码，所以「包 = 清单点名的文件」是安全的，也和我们现有白名单（`server/http/static.js:89` 只服务清单点名的文件）一致。这是**有意偏离 Forge**，理由要写进文档。\n\n---\n\n## 2. 目标 / 非目标\n\n### 目标（P1 + P2）\n- `data` 类型成为 **supported**：`packs/<id>/pack.json` + 它点名的数据文件 → 启动时校验并叠加进服务端内存数据；浏览器取同一批文件、用同一份代码本地合并 → **两端数据逐字节同源**。\n- 只增不改：官方记录要改必须写 `overrides`；非法/未知/冲突项**点名报告且不生效**，绝不静默。\n- 每个已装包可作为**房间级开关**（复用现有 mod 开关），房间关掉它时，它的记录从该房间的数据里消失。\n- 内容哈希进握手（`welcome` / `/healthz` / BattleSpec + UI 标记），客户端在「准备」前核对本地包字节。\n- 工具与文档：`tools/packs.mjs list/check` 对 data 类型给出与装载器**同一套**判据；`docs/PACKS.md` §4 改成 supported 并写清角色、字段、检查、规则。\n\n### 非目标（明确不做，且理由进文档）\n- **任何执行第三方代码的格子**：`kits/*.js`、`server.meta`、`server.modules`、`server.battle`、`server.room`、`client.panels`、`routes`、`assets` 容器、`forms`/`fx` 渲染增量。\n- **素材类型**（`assets` 保持 planned）：包不能带立绘/语音（`voices`/`voiceLangs`）/时装（`skins`）/图标映射（`bondIcons`/`itemIcons`/`art`）。⇒ P1 的 data 包只能带**规则与数据**；新增干员没有立绘（退化表现照旧由客户端处理）。\n- Forge 的 ABI 概念（`api` / `MOD_API_VERSION`）：**没有代码就没有 ABI**；我们只保留已有的 `app` 版本区间（`docs/PACKS.md:62`）。\n- 不新建第二套发现/索引/下载系统：`/packs/index.json` + `/packs/<id>/<file>` 就是我们的 `modCatalog` + 下载通道。\n- 包不写 `data/*.json`；包不进 golden；本机第三方包不进发布包。\n\n---\n\n## 3. 设计\n\n### 3.1 数据流\n\n```\n服务端（启动一次，live:false）\n  server/packs.js 扫 packs/  →  对每个 type=data 的包：\n      读 pack.json → normalizeManifest(新 data 分支)      [shared/packs.js]\n      读它点名的每个数据文件 → normalizeDataFile(...)     [shared/dataPacks.js]\n      冲突/非法项 → report（点名 + 理由），坏的整条/整包不生效\n      digest = modManifestDigest(声明文件排序后的 {path,hash})   [shared/modIdentity 的同构实现]\n  server/data.js loadData():  deepFreeze 之前 applyDataPacks(out, packs) → out\n      日志：[packs] applied N data pack(s): … （+ 每条 error/warning）\n  server/lobby.js dataFor(room):  filterData(base, room.mods)   ← 已有\n      （P2：包按 id 注册成 mod；关掉的包其记录从该房间数据里摘除）\n\n浏览器（P1：同一份数据，自己合并）\n  public/js/data.js createDataStore():\n      取 /data/<file>.json            ← 已有\n      取 /packs/index.json            ← 已有路由，data 类型 supported 后自然出现\n      取每个已装/已启用包的声明文件    ← 已有路由（白名单）\n      applyDataPacks(...)             ← 与服端同一份 shared/dataPacks.js\n      校验 digest（与 welcome 里的一致才允许「准备」）\n      filterData(..., room.mods)      ← 已有\n```\n\n### 3.2 模块与接口草案\n\n| 模块 | 动作 | 关键接口 |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `shared/dataPacks.js`（新增） | Forge 数据规则的**裁剪版** | `DATA_PACK_FILES`（角色闭集）、`ID_FIELD_BY_FILE`、`DATA_PACK_FIELDS`（清单字段闭集）、`OVERRIDE_ENTRY_RE`、`parseDataManifest(raw, ctx)`、`normalizeDataFile(role, raw, {overrides, official})`、`applyDataPacks(data, packs)` → `{ data, report }`、`dataPackSummary(report)`、`dataPackDigest(files)`、错误码常量 |\n| `shared/packs.js` | 打开 `data` 类型 | `PACK_TYPES.data = { status:'supported', live:false, files:{ chess:{ext:['.json']}, bonds:…, units/backups:…, items, enemies, stages, waves, tokens, bosses, factions, garrisons, bands, effects, choices, hextech } }`；`normalizeManifest` 加 data 分支（`overrides`、`content` 派生自 `files`）；`packIndexEntry` 加 `digest` |\n| `server/packs.js` | 类型感知装载 | 对 `data` 包做一次解析并缓存（`live:false`）；导出 `dataPacks()`（规范化后的包列表 + report + digest）；`servable()` 不变（白名单已按角色） |\n| `server/data.js` | 叠加 seam | `loadData(dir, { log, expected, packsDir = PACKS_DIR, dataPacks = null })`，在 `:81 deepFreeze` 前 `applyDataPacks`；`packsDir: null` / `dataPacks: []` ⇒ **逐字节等于今天**（测试与 golden 的安全默认） |\n| `public/js/data.js` | 客户端合并 | `createDataStore({ base, packsUrl = '/packs/index.json' })`：拉官方文件后拉 data 包并按同序合并；失败时**降级为不带包**并给出可见提示，绝不静默用半份数据开打 |\n| `shared/protocol.js` + `server/lobby.js` | 握手 | `welcome.mods`（Forge 叫 `welcome.mods {digest, packs}`）承载**内容包摘要**；`room.state` 带同一份；BattleSpec 记入（`docs/PACKS.md:141-142` 的硬要求） |\n| `shared/mods.js` | P2：动态注册 | 每个 data 包 → 一个 mod id（建议 `pack:<packId>`，与现有 id 空间不冲突），`apply` = 按合并期记录的 per-pack 索引摘除它加/覆盖的记录；`hidden` 不适用（玩家看得见）；默认开 |\n| `tools/packs.mjs` | 工具 | `list`/`check` 对 data 包给出与装载器同一判据（复用 `parseDataManifest` + 一次 dry-run `applyDataPacks`）；`--strict` 在有问题时退出 1 |\n| 文档 | 必须同步 | `docs/PACKS.md` §1/§2/§4/§6、`docs/MODS.md`（关系）、`docs/DATA.md`（若提到生成数据不可改）、`CHANGELOG.md`；`test/docs-consistency.test.js` / `docs-paths` 要绿 |\n\n### 3.3 角色（`files`）的提议名单\n\n对齐我们的 `server/data.js:27-31 DATA_FILES`，**去掉 `config`（规则，Forge 同样刻意排除）**、**去掉 `assets`（美术清单 → 属于未来的 assets 类型）**、**加上 `hextech`（0.3.0 自有内容）**：\n\n```\nchess  bonds  garrisons  items  bands  effects  choices  enemies  factions\nwaves  stages  bosses  tokens  backups(units 记录)  hextech\n```\n\n`backups` 的处理照 Forge 的先例（`docs/WORKSHOP.md:42-45`：`units.json` 与落点不同名）——我们这里角色名可以直接叫 `units`，落点写 `backups.units`，并在文档里点名说明，避免作者踩坑。\n\n---\n\n## 4. 分期计划\n\n### P1 —— data 类型落地 + 两端同源（不含房间子集）\n1. `shared/dataPacks.js`：清单/内容文件规范化 + 合并 + 摘要 + 报告（含全部错误码）。\n2. `shared/packs.js`：`data` → supported + 角色表 + `digest` 进索引。\n3. `server/packs.js`：启动装载 data 包（`live:false`），暴露给 `server/data.js`。\n4. `server/data.js`：叠加 seam + 日志（`[packs] applied N data pack(s)` + 每条 error/warning）。\n5. `public/js/data.js`：拉 `/packs/index.json` + 包文件 + 同码合并 + 摘要核对。\n6. `shared/protocol.js` / `server/lobby.js`：`welcome.mods`（内容包摘要）与 UI「已加装内容」标记。\n7. `tools/packs.mjs`：`list/check` 支持 data 类型。\n8. 测试 + 文档（见 §5）。\n\n**P1 验收**\n```\nnode tools/packs.mjs list                 # 一个 fixture data 包被识别为 supported，坏包被点名跳过\nnode tools/packs.mjs check --strict       # 坏包/警告 ⇒ 退出 1\nnode test/data-packs.test.js              # 规范化/合并/覆盖门/空包/摘要稳定性/坏包不拖垮\nnode test/i18n-packs.test.js              # 既有包机制不回归\nnode test/mods.test.js                    # 房间过滤不回归\nnode test/golden.test.js                  # 必须与今天一致（包不进 golden）\nnode tools/build-hextech.mjs --check      # 生成数据仍与生成器一致\n```\n（本沙箱 `node --test` 会 `spawn EPERM`，逐文件 `node <testfile>` 跑。）\n\n### P2 —— 房间子集 + 准备前对齐\n1. `shared/mods.js`：data 包 → 动态 mod id；`apply` 按 per-pack 索引摘除；默认开、可 `SP_MODS`/房间开关关闭。\n2. 房间/大厅 UI：把包作为可开关项列出（复用 `ModsPanel` 与 `VISIBLE_MOD_IDS` 的渲染路径；包数量可能多，考虑折叠）。\n3. 客户端对齐：`准备` 前核对本地包摘要与 `room.state.mods`，缺文件/摘要不符 ⇒ 不给准备按钮并提示重新拉取。\n4. `tools/package.mjs` / `.gitignore`：明确第三方包的打包策略（见 §6）。\n\n**P2 验收**：两台客户端 + 一个房间端到端（一个装包、一个不装 ⇒ 后者被拦在准备之前）；`test/mods.test.js` 增加「两台服务器/两个房间内容不同」的包版用例（现有测试已有同型用例可仿）。\n\n### P3（可选，非承诺）—— 包的导入/导出与脚手架\n需要 zip 能力：**我们树里目前没有 `shared/zip.js`**（对比 Forge 有 435 行、且明确处理 ZIP64/加密/多卷/重名/CRC/GBK 文件名）。\n两条路：(a) 搬 Forge 的 `shared/zip.js` 并裁剪；(b) 包只以**目录**形式分发（`packs/<id>/`），导入/导出交给用户自己解压/压缩。\n建议先只做 (b)：功能不受影响，风险最低；`tools/packs.mjs check` 已经能在目录形态上做全部校验。\n\n---\n\n## 5. 文件级工作量（P1）\n\n| 文件 | 动作 | 估计 |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `shared/dataPacks.js` | 新增：清单/内容规范化 + 合并 + 摘要 + 报告 | 600–900 行 |\n| `shared/packs.js` | `data` 类型条目、data 清单分支、`digest` 进索引 | +80–140 |\n| `server/packs.js` | data 包启动装载、缓存、导出 | +60–120 |\n| `server/data.js` | 叠加 seam + 日志 | +20–30 |\n| `public/js/data.js` | 拉包 + 同码合并 + 摘要核对 | +40–80 |\n| `shared/protocol.js` | `welcome.mods` 校验分支 | +20 |\n| `server/lobby.js` | `welcome` 里带上内容包摘要 | +20 |\n| `tools/packs.mjs` | data 类型的 list/check | +80–120 |\n| `test/data-packs.test.js` | 新增测试 | 350–500 行 |\n| `docs/PACKS.md` / `docs/MODS.md` / `CHANGELOG.md` | 文档同步 | 150–250 行 |\n| **合计** | | **≈1,300–1,900 行** |\n\nP2 追加：`shared/mods.js` +120–200、UI +60–120、客户端对齐 +60–120、`tools/package.mjs`/`.gitignore` +20–40、测试 +150–250。**全程无新依赖。**\n\n---\n\n## 6. 需要业主拍板的四件事\n\n1. **第三方包要不要提交进 git？** 我们的 `packs/` 是「提交才随发布包走」（`docs/PACKS.md:161-163`）。Forge 的工坊包是**本机目录**、不进仓库。建议：`packs/<id>/` 里只提交我们认可的包；第三方包默认 `.gitignore`（或放一个不打包的目录），与现机制一致。\n2. **内容哈希什么时候上？** 我们自己的文档要求「data 包上线**之前**」就有（`docs/PACKS.md:141-142`）。建议 P1 就做（摘要 + `welcome` + UI 标记），否则等于带着一个已知的一致性缺口上线。\n3. **素材（`assets` 类型）要不要跟着做？** 不做的话 data 包只能带规则、不能带立绘/图标（新干员会是「无立绘」的退化表现）。做的话是**另一套类型**（只读文件 + 清单，仍然不执行代码），工作量与风险都要单独评估。\n4. **房间子集（P2）是否必要？** 若只想要「服务器整体加装内容」，P1 就够；但那样就退回到 Forge 的「本体含全部已装包」，与我们 `docs/MODS.md` 的「房间级开关」哲学不完全一致。\n\n---\n\n## 7. 风险与坑\n\n| 风险 | 说明与对策 |\n| --- | --- |\n| **两端数据同源** | 最大的一条：浏览器静态取 `/data/`，服务端内存叠加。P1 必须让浏览器用同一份 shared 代码合并；摘要核对是兜底，不是替代 |\n| 确定性/顺序 | 包的叠加顺序必须固定（建议：包 id 字典序；同一文件内记录按 id 排序），否则两端摘要与推演会漂移 |\n| golden | 包的装载默认必须是「关」：`loadData({packsDir: null})` 等价于今天；golden 只跑原生内容（我们 doc 的硬要求） |\n| 打包/更新 | `tools/package.mjs` 的 `URL_MOUNTS` 已含 `/packs/`，但**只带已提交包**；第三方包若被误提交会进入所有客户端（Forge 的等价教训见 `docs/WORKSHOP.md:490-492`） |\n| 版本区间 | 我们已有 `app` 区间（`docs/PACKS.md:62`）；Forge 额外的 `api`/`game` 只保留 `game` 这类「针对哪个上游版本」的信息即可，不要引入 ABI |\n| 工具链 | `tools/packs.mjs check` 与装载器必须用**同一个**规范化函数，否则会出现「check 通过、装不上」 |\n| 测试沙箱 | 本机 `node --test` 必然 `spawn EPERM`；验收一律 `node <testfile>`，CI 侧不受影响 |\n| 文档约束 | 新增文档页/引用尚未存在的模块可能触发 `test/docs-consistency.test.js` / `docs-paths`；`docs/PACKS.md` 的改动要与 `shared/packs.js` 同批 |\n| 偏离 Forge 的自觉 | 「包 = 清单点名的文件」（不是 Forge 的「目录全部文件」）是**有意**偏离，理由（我们不执行包代码）必须写进 `docs/PACKS.md` |\n\n---\n\n## 8. 附录：Forge 能力逐格对照\n\n| Forge 格子 | 我们搬吗 | 落点 / 理由 |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| `workshop/` 目录 + 独立注册表 | ❌ | 用现有 `packs/` + `server/packs.js`（`docs/PACKS.md:74-80`） |\n| `pack.json` 清单校验（闭集键） | ✅（裁剪） | 456:  * Zip `cwd`/FOLDER into `zipPath`: Info-ZIP `zip`, else a bsdtar `tar --format zip` (Windows 10+, macOS). bsdtar writes
457:  * the names in UTF-8 (flag bit 11) only when told: its default is the system code page, which loses a name it cannot
458:  * spell (卫戍 becomes ?? on Windows code page 1252) and leaves no UTF-8 copy for a reader.
459:  */
460: export function zipFolder(cwd, zipPath) {
461:   // deflate everything: storing PNG / MP3 as they are made the full zip 8.5 MB larger and saved no real time
462:   const z = spawnSync('zip', ['-q', '-r', '-X', zipPath, FOLDER], { cwd, stdio: 'inherit' });
463:   if (!z.error && z.status === 0) return;
464:   const t = spawnSync('tar', ['-c', '--format', 'zip', '--options', 'zip:hdrcharset=UTF-8', '-f', zipPath, FOLDER], { cwd, stdio: 'inherit' });
465:   if (t.error || t.status !== 0 || !isFile(zipPath)) throw new Error('zipping failed: install `zip` (or a bsdtar `tar`)');
466: }
467: 
468: const isLib = (f) => f.startsWith('node_modules/') || f.startsWith('public/vendor/');
469: const META_FILES = new Set([MANIFEST_FILE, UPDATE_FILE]);
470: const HASHES = /[0-9a-f]{64}/g;
471: 
472: /**
473:  * What is wrong with a stage before it is zipped: a file not planned, a planned one missing (node_modules and
474:  * public/vendor, npm ci's output, only count when `libsPlanned`), a refused path, personal info (home paths and names
475:  * in every file, names only in the libraries; MANIFEST.json / UPDATE.json without their sha256 values).
476:  * @param {string} stage
477:  * @param {Set<string>} planned
478:  * @returns {{ staged: string[], problems: string[] }}
479:  */
480: export function stageProblems(stage, planned, { env = process.env, libsPlanned = false } = {}) {
481:   const problems = [];
482:   const staged = listTree(stage, '', { junk: true });
483:   const held = libsPlanned ? staged : staged.filter((f) => !isLib(f));
484:   for (const f of held) if (!planned.has(f)) problems.push(`in the stage but not planned: ${f}`);
485:   const have = new Set(held);
486:   for (const f of planned) if (!have.has(f)) problems.push(`planned but not staged: ${f}`);
487:   for (const r of REFUSE) if (fs.existsSync(path.join(stage, r))) problems.push(`refused: ${r} is in the stage`);
488:   const names = scanNames(env);
489:   const rest = staged.filter((f) => !isLib(f));
490:   for (const h of scanFiles(stage, rest.filter((f) => !META_FILES.has(f)), { home: true, names })) problems.push(`personal info: ${h}`);
491:   for (const h of scanFiles(stage, rest.filter((f) => META_FILES.has(f)), { home: true, names, strip: HASHES })) problems.push(`personal info: ${h}`);
492:   for (const h of scanFiles(stage, staged.filter(isLib), { home: false, names })) problems.push(`personal info: ${h}`);
493:   return { staged, problems };
494: }

(Showing lines 455-494 of 759. Use offset=495 to continue.)

`zipFolder` shells out to `zip`/`tar` (external binaries) — so it's a writer but depends on system tools (and in this sandbox spawnSync pipes are blocked). For pack export in P3, we could reuse `readZip` (pure Node, in-process) and either shell out for writing (like releases do) or write a minimal store-only zip writer in Node (easy: ~80 lines, since pack files are JSON/text; Forge's workshop-pack.mjs uses deterministic deflate + fixed DOS timestamps for reproducibility).

1. P3: zip capability partially exists: `readZip` in tools/package-update.mjs:62 (pure Node, zip64/deflate/UTF-8/0x7075/CRC/symlink guard) — so **import** is nearly free; **export** today shells out (`tools/package.mjs:460 zipFolder` uses Info-ZIP `zip` else bsdtar `tar`), so a Node writer (~80-120 lines, deterministic timestamps) would be needed if we want export without external tools.

## 2. DATA-ONLY pack.json FIELD SET

`PACK_FIELDS` (shared/workshop.js:111-128) is a CLOSED set of 30 keys; any other top-level key refuses the whole pack with `PACK_UNKNOWN_FIELD` (1438-1442). Normalized object returned at 1936-1969. Always-present keys (22): id, name, version, author, license, hasAssets, description, gameVersion, api, game, layer, combat, content, overrides, voices, voiceLangs, bondIcons, itemIcons, art, support, operators, playtest. Conditional keys (`...Object.fromEntries(declared)`, 1817-1826 + 1967): assets, client, server, routes, i18n, notices, forms, fx — **only when the key is literally present in pack.json** (this is load-bearing for hashes, see §4). `skins` only when non-empty (1961).

PURE DATA fields and where records land:
- `content[]` (1448-1450) — whitelist-filtered to `WORKSHOP_CONTENT_FILES` (chess, units, items, enemies, stages, waves, tokens, bosses, factions, garrisons, bands, bonds, effects, choices; `config` deliberately excluded, 26-30), deduped + `.sort()`. Each entry `f` → loader reads `<f>.json` → `normalizeContentFile` → `pack.files[f]` → overlay `data[f]`, except `units` → `data.backups.units` (`OVERLAY_TARGET_BY_FILE` 2579).
- `overrides[]` (1453-1455) — filtered by `OVERRIDE_ENTRY_RE` (140) `^([a-z]+):([A-Za-z0-9_\-.:]{1,64})$`, deduped+sorted; participates ONLY in the merge decision (2683) — it is never written into data.
- `voices` (1470-1510) → `assets.audio.voice` via `workshopVoiceIndex`/`mergeWorkshopVoices` (2887, 2264-2288); URLs are `WORKSHOP_MEDIA_PREFIX + packId + '/' + percent-encoded path`.
- `voiceLangs` (1516-1539) → `assets.audio.voiceLangs[lang]`, key order forced to `VOICE_LANGS` order (1539).
- `bondIcons` (1546-1560) → `assets.bonds[bondId]`.
- `itemIcons` (1568-1582) → `assets.items[itemId]`.
- `art` (1598-1625) → `assets.<table>` (chars/enemies/tokens) or, for flat tables (skills/profSub/bonds/bands, `ART_TABLES` 1287-1309), `assets.skills` / `assets.prof.sub` / `assets.bonds` / `assets.bands` via `mergeWorkshopFlatArt` 3422.
- `skins[]` (1644-1706) → `assets.skins[charId]` (mergeWorkshopSkins 3194).
- `support[]` (1711-1721) → `data.support.pool[<tier>]` (mergeWorkshopSupport 2536; tier DERIVED from the merged chess record, never written).
- `operators{}` (1732-1750) → `data.backups.diy.ownedPool` + `diy.operators[charId]` (mergeWorkshopOperators 3613); name/rarity/profession/subProfessionId DERIVED from the pack's own `units[charId]` (3520-3596).
- `i18n{}` (1794, parser 1075-1114) → merged `/i18n/<code>.json` bodies (workshopI18nFiles 2134) — NOT part of the data overlay.
- `notices{}` (1798, parser 1051-1073) → `data/notices.json` body (server/notices.js) — merged by server/index.js:154-155, not by applyWorkshop.
- `forms{}` (1803-1811, parser 1116-1189) → `assets.modForms` (mergeWorkshopForms 3274); first-party FORMS win.
- `fx.silentPhases[]` (1812, parser 1212-1243) → `assets.silentPhases` (mergeWorkshopFx 3335).
- `playtest.directToHand[]` (1761-1778) → **no data file**; read only by the playtest server (`SP_PLAYTEST=1`). Explicitly NOT a contribution (1844, 1855).
- Identity/meta scalars `id,name,version,author,license,description,gameVersion,game,api,layer,combat` (1443-1456, 1888-1913, 1938-1950). `game` is the upstream-version range, `gameVersion` its alias (`game` wins, 1908); `api` is the mod-API range checked against `MOD_API_VERSION` (1903).

EXCLUDED and why:
- `server` (parser 780-853) — four sub-decls, every one names a `.mjs` the server `await import()`s: `preDispatch.module`+`policy`, `meta.module` (+`registers`), `modules[].entry` (+`uses`,`write`), `battle.module`, `room.module`. Pure code execution. 780-1970's gates: `META_NEEDS_COMBAT` 1918, `BATTLE_NEEDS_COMBAT` 1922, `MODULES_NEED_COMBAT` 1928.
- `client` — half code: `panels[].module` (.js loaded into the browser), `panels[].styles` (.css injected), `wraps` (engine component wrap/replace), `data`/`messages` (panel's data & channel grants). Parser 501-713. The `client.theme.vars` sub-part IS pure data (CSS custom properties) and `client.require` is a capability list — but both live inside the same key, so a data-only port should refuse the whole `client` key rather than half-read it.
- `assets` (parser 437-468) — pure **asset container** plumbing: `container` (`.spresources`), `manifest` (`.json`), `serverPolicy`, `verify`. Its only data contribution is the synthetic hash row `assets.container.sha256` (§4), which exists to bind the container bytes into the pack hash. Excluded because it ships a container, not records.
- `routes` (parser 985-1049) — a borderline: it is genuinely DATA (`.json` only, `ROUTE_BAD_FILE` at 1006 refuses anything but `.json`) but it is not merged into the loaded data; it is a per-pack HTTP surface (`public`/`no-cache`/`no-store`, 428-430) served by static.js. Keep or drop as a policy choice; it never executes.

## 3. MERGE / OVERLAY RULES

Core rule (file header 14-16): **additive by default; an official id is replaced ONLY if the pack lists `"<file>:<id>"` in `overrides`.**
- `applyWorkshop(base, packs)` shared/workshop.js:2647-2759. Pure: builds a new top object (`{...base}`, 2648), never mutates input.
- **Ordering is NOT by layer.** It is `byPackId` — code-unit compare on pack id, ascending (2321), applied at 2661: `[].filter(...).sort(byPackId)`. Smaller id wins every collision. Layer A/B/C never participates in merge order.
- Per record, in `overlayContentFile`'s `mergeOne` callback (2668-2708): four ordered decisions —
  1. `contributors` map hit → `PACK_ID_COLLISION` (2676-2682): another PACK already contributed `"<file>:<id>"`; later pack loses even if it declared the override. `definedBy` = holder.
  2. `exists && !declared.has(key)` → `OFFICIAL_ID_COLLISION` (2683-2689), record kept.
  3. `exists` → `unknownOverrideKeys(baseContainerFor(base,file)?.[id] ?? priorMap[id], rec)` (2693-2701) → `UNKNOWN_OVERRIDE_FIELD` if the patch names a field the record lacks ("closed world").
  4. else → added. Result record: `exists ? mergeRecord(priorMap[id], rec) : rec` (2707) — note it merges onto **priorMap[id]** (official OR an earlier pack's record), not onto `base`.
- `overlayContentFile` 2596-2620: path = `OVERLAY_TARGET_BY_FILE[file] || [file]`; each path segment is shallow-copied and re-attached (2618) so sibling keys under `backups` (`diy`, `tokens`) are untouched. Returns `{added, overridden}`.
- `mergeRecord(base, patch)` 2417-2430 — FIELD-LEVEL, recursive; non-mutating. Per key: `OVERRIDE_KEYED_LISTS` key + both arrays → `mergeKeyedList`; else `OVERRIDE_REPLACE_KEYS.includes(key) || Array.isArray(value)` → **replace wholesale**; else recursive merge for plain objects, else take the value. `OVERRIDE_REPLACE_KEYS` = `['skill','skills','trait','traitBase','traitOverride','modules','rangeGrid','attackRangeGrid','assets','diy','bonds']` (2378-2381).
- `OVERRIDE_KEYED_LISTS` = `{talents:'index', talentsBase:'index', talentChanges:'talentIndex'}` (2407). `mergeKeyedList` 2447-2470: pairs by integer id key, base order preserved, unmatched patch entries appended; **if either side has a duplicate key, the whole list falls back to wholesale replace** (2449-2458) — because `talentChanges` uses `-1` for hidden module talents.
- `unknownOverrideKeys` 2479-2482: `Object.keys(patch).filter((k) => !Object.hasOwn(base, k))`.
- Per-content-file normalizer `normalizeContentFile(file, json)` 2012-2033: must be a plain object (`BAD_CONTENT`); every key matches `RECORD_ID_RE` (169) = `^[A-Za-z0-9_\-.:]{1,64}$` (`BAD_RECORD_ID`); every value a plain object (`BAD_RECORD`); when `ID_FIELD_BY_FILE[file]` (33-37: chess→chessId, units→charId, items→id, enemies→key, stages→stageId, waves→templateId, tokens→tokenId, bosses→bossId, factions→factionId, garrisons→garrisonId, bands→bandId, bonds→bondId, effects→effectId, choices→id) is present on the record it must EQUAL the key (`ID_MISMATCH`, 2020-2022); for `units` additionally all six `UNIT_REQUIRED_FIELDS` (49-86) must pass (`UNIT_MISSING_CHAR_ID`, `UNIT_MISSING_NAME`, `UNIT_BAD_RARITY`, `UNIT_MISSING_PROFESSION`, `UNIT_MISSING_SUB_PROFESSION`, `UNIT_BAD_FORMS`); zero records → `EMPTY_CONTENT` (2031).
- Dedup/order normalisation everywhere: `[...new Set(...)].sort()` for `content` (1448) and `overrides` (1453); `stableStringList` (208) for date-ish lists; `orderedOperators` sorted by charId (1750); `directToHand.sort()` (1777); `parseStringList` sorts (1261); voice file lists `[...new Set(list)].sort()` (1502); `orderedLangLines` forced to `VOICE_LANGS` order (1539).
- `EMPTY_PACK` (1847-1862): after ALL contributions are parsed. Contribution = any `content[]`, `voices`, `voiceLangs`, `bondIcons`, `itemIcons`, `art`, `skins`, `operators`, or any of the 8 `declared` keys that `contributesDecl` counts (`fx.silentPhases: []` deliberately does NOT count, 1840). `support` and `playtest` are named in the message as NOT contributions.
- Post-record merges, in this exact order (2713-2738 — order is load-bearing and commented): `linkWorkshopStages` → `mergeWorkshopVoices` → `mergeWorkshopBondIcons` → `mergeWorkshopItemIcons` → `mergeWorkshopArt` → `mergeWorkshopFlatArt` → `mergeWorkshopSkins` → `mergeWorkshopForms` → `mergeWorkshopFx` → `mergeWorkshopSupport` → `mergeWorkshopOperators`.
- `linkWorkshopStages` 3658-3678: for each newly added stage, appends its id to the `config.modes[m].stages` lists its own `modes[]` names. `config` is otherwise never contributed.
- `report` shape (2649): `{packs:[{id,name,files:{<file>:{added,overridden}}}], added:{}, overridden:{}, errors:[], looks:[], overlaps, strippedPool, poolStripped, support, operators, voiceLangs, bondIcons, itemIcons, art, skinsByPack, flatArt, linkedStages, supportOff}` — `added`/`overridden` lists sorted at 2756-2757.

FULL ERROR/WARNING CODE LIST (with meaning):

*Shape layer, whole-pack refusal* (shared/workshop.js): `BAD_MANIFEST` 1434 (pack.json not an object); `PACK_UNKNOWN_FIELD` 1440 (top-level key outside `PACK_FIELDS`); `BAD_PACK_ID` 1444; `PACK_ID_MISMATCH` 1446 (pack.json id ≠ directory name); `ASSETS_NEED_LICENSE` 1462 (`assets/` exists but no `license`); `EMPTY_PACK` 1862; `BAD_API_TYPE` 1888 / `BAD_GAME_TYPE` 1890+1894 (declared non-string); `BAD_API_RANGE` 1897; `MOD_API_INCOMPATIBLE` 1904 (`api` excludes `MOD_API_VERSION`); `BAD_GAME_RANGE` 1909; `BAD_LAYER` 1912; `BAD_COMBAT` 1913; `META_NEEDS_COMBAT` 1919; `BATTLE_NEEDS_COMBAT` 1923; `MODULES_NEED_COMBAT` 1929.
